Now, let's plan the code structure.

```python
import sys
from collections import deque

class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.ad = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        self.ad[fr].append([to, cap, len(self.ad[to])])
        self.ad[to].append([fr, 0, len(self.ad[fr])-1])
    def bfs(self, s, t):
        level = [-1]*self.n
        q = deque([s])
        level[s]=0
        while q:
            v=q.popleft()
            for e in self.ad[v]:
                if e[1] > 0 and level[e[0]] < 0:
                    level[e[0]] = level[v]+1
                    q.append(e[0])
        self.level = level
        return level[t] >= 0
    def dfs(self, v, t, f):
        if v == t:
            return f
        for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.ad[v])):
            self.it[v] = i
            e = self.ad[v][i]
            if e[1] > 0 and self.level[v] < self.level[e[0]]:
                ret = self.dfs(e[0], t, min(f, e[1]))
                if ret:
                    e[1] -= ret
                    self.ad[e[0]][e[2]][1] += ret
                    return ret
        return 0
    def max_flow(self, s, t):
        flow = 0
        INF = 10**18
        while self.bfs(s,t):
            self.it = [0]*self.n
            while True:
                f = self.dfs(s,t,INF)
                if not f:
                    break
                flow += f
        return flow
```

Need be careful with recursive DFS depth: nodes <=28, fine.

Alternatively, use a simpler Ford-Fulkerson with DFS since capacities small; Dinic okay.

Now, SCC computation:

```python
def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data: return
    N = int(data[0])
    edges = []
    reach = [[False]*26 for _ in range(26)]
    for i in range(26):
        reach[i][i] = True
    for i in range(N):
        s = data[1+i]
        u = ord(s[0]) - ord('A')
        v = ord(s[1]) - ord('A')
        edges.append((u,v))
        reach[u][v] = True
    # Floyd
    for k in range(26):
        for i in range(26):
            if reach[i][k]:
                for j in range(26):
                    if reach[k][j]:
                        reach[i][j] = True
    comp = [-1]*26
    cid = 0
    for i in range(26):
        if comp[i] == -1:
            comp[i] = cid
            for j in range(26):
                if comp[j] == -1 and reach[i][j] and reach[j][i]:
                    comp[j] = cid
            cid += 1
    C = cid
    internal = [False]*C
    indeg = [0]*C
    outdeg = [0]*C
    inter_pairs = set()
    for u,v in edges:
        cu = comp[u]; cv = comp[v]
        if cu == cv:
            internal[cu] = True
        else:
            outdeg[cu] += 1
            indeg[cv] += 1
            inter_pairs.add((cu, cv))
    b = [indeg[i] - outdeg[i] for i in range(C)]
    P0 = sum(x for x in b if x > 0)
    # flow network
    S = C; T = C+1
    dinic = Dinic(C+2)
    INF = 10**9
    for i in range(C):
        if b[i] > 0:
            dinic.add_edge(S, i, b[i])
        elif b[i] < 0:
            dinic.add_edge(i, T, -b[i])
    for u,v in inter_pairs:
        dinic.add_edge(u, v, INF)
    M = dinic.max_flow(S, T)
    C_paths = P0 - M
    Q = 0
    for i in range(C):
        if internal[i] and indeg[i] == 0 and outdeg[i] == 0:
            Q += 1
    print(C_paths + Q)
```

Now, let's think about if the Eulerian trail decomposition of f after max flow might produce a trail that starts at an SCC with internal edges and no outgoing inter edge? In f, a trail starts at a vertex with b'>0 (in_f - out_f >0? Wait trail starts where out_f - in_f >0? Let's check. For a directed multigraph, the number of trails starting at v is max(0, out_f - in_f) = max(0, -b'_v). Earlier I used b' = in_f - out_f. Positive b' means more incoming than outgoing, so trails end there. The number of trails = sum positive b' = sum max(0, in_f - out_f). A trail starts at a vertex with out_f - in_f >0, i.e., b'<0. So in the decomposition, starts are at negative b' vertices, ends at positive b' vertices. This matches original f=1 path A->B->C: b_A=-1, b_C=1, trail starts at A (negative b) and ends at C (positive b). Good. So after max flow, b' positive at some vertices, negative at others. The number of trails is sum positive b' = P0-M. If an SCC with internal edges has b' negative (out_f > in_f), a trail starts there; we can cover internal edges. If b' positive, trail ends there; cover internal edges. If b'=0, trails pass through; cover internal edges. Good.

We argued no because max flow only sends from positive to negative, so a negative vertex receives at most its demand -b_v, so b'_v <=0. A positive vertex sends at most its supply, so b'_v >=0. Zero remains zero. Thus signs don't flip. Good.

A path can cover at most one outgoing edge from a source. Thus number of paths >= total outdeg of source SCCs. This is a valid lower bound. In sample1 inter DAG source C1 out1, X isolated not in inter DAG; C(D)>=1. Answer includes Q for X. Good.
Could max flow reduce C below total source outdeg? No, because source b<0, not positive, so no flow starts at source; P0-M is at least total source outdeg? Now, let's think about if the max flow value M can be found by a simpler algorithm: Since graph is small, we could also compute all-pairs reachability in condensation DAG, then solve max flow on a bipartite graph with supplies/demands. But Dinic on 28 nodes is simpler.

Now, let's ensure the Dinic implementation handles the reverse edge indices correctly. In `add_edge`, we store `[to, cap, rev]`. In DFS, when we push flow, we update `e[1] -= ret` and reverse edge `self.ad[e[0]][e[2]][1] += ret`. This is standard. Need set `self.it[v] = i` before recursing? The typical Dinic:
```
for i in range(self.it[v], len(g[v])):
    self.it[v] = i
    e = g[v][i]
    if e.cap > 0 and level[v] < level[e.to]:
        d = dfs(e.to, t, min(f, e.cap))
        if d > 0:
            e.cap -= d
            g[e.to][e.rev].cap += d
            return d
self.it[v] = len(g[v])
return 0
```
Our loop with `self.it[v] = i` at start of iteration and not incrementing after failed edge? We set `self.it[v] = i`, then if edge fails, loop increments i and sets it to next. This is okay. But if an edge returns ret, we return without incrementing; next DFS will try same edge because it may still have capacity. That's fine. Need ensure `self.it[v]` is updated to i before recursion. Good.

Alternatively, use a simpler Edmonds-Karp BFS since nodes small and max flow ≤676. But Dinic okay.

Let's write a clean Dinic:

```python
class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.g = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        fwd = [to, cap, None]
        rev = [fr, 0, fwd]
        fwd[2] = rev
        self.g[fr].append(fwd)
        self.g[to].append(rev)
```
Using list references can be tricky. Better use indices.

```python
class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.g = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        self.g[fr].append([to, cap, len(self.g[to])])
        self.g[to].append([fr, 0, len(self.g[fr])-1])
```
This is standard.

BFS:
```python
def bfs(self,s,t):
    self.level = [-1]*self.n
    q = deque([s])
    self.level[s]=0
    while q:
        v=q.popleft()
        for to, cap, rev in self.g[v]:
            if cap > 0 and self.level[to] < 0:
                self.level[to] = self.level[v]+1
                q.append(to)
    return self.level[t] >= 0
```
DFS:
```python
def dfs(self,v,t,f):
    if v==t: return f
    for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.g[v])):
        self.it[v] = i
        e = self.g[v][i]
        to, cap, rev = e
        if cap > 0 and self.level[v] < self.level[to]:
            ret = self.dfs(to,t,min(f,cap))
            if ret:
                e[1] -= ret
                self.g[to][rev][1] += ret
                return ret
    return 0
```
Need if edge fails, loop continues; but `self.it[v] = i` remains, and next iteration sets to i+1. If recursion returns 0, we continue. At end return 0. This is fine. However, if an edge has cap>0 but dfs returns 0, we should increment `it[v]` to skip it for this BFS phase. In the loop, after dfs returns 0, the for loop will increment i and set it[v] to next. So okay. If dfs returns ret, we return without incrementing; next call may try same edge again if still cap. That's okay.

Max_flow:
```python
def max_flow(self,s,t):
    flow=0
    INF=10**18
    while self.bfs(s,t):
        self.it=[0]*self.n
        while True:
            f=self.dfs(s,t,INF)
            if not f: break
            flow += f
    return flow
```

Now, let's double-check the max flow formula with the LP objective one more time.

We want minimize Σ max(0, in_f - out_f) over f_e >=1. Let b_v = in_1 - out_1. Let g=f-1. Then in_f - out_f = b_v + in_g - out_g = b_v - (out_g - in_g) = b_v - h_v. We want minimize Σ max(0, b_v - h_v). Since g is a nonnegative flow, h is its divergence. Decompose g into paths. Each path from s to t contributes h_s +=1, h_t -=1. The change in objective from this unit is:
Δ = max(0, b_s -1) - max(0,b_s) + max(0,b_t +1) - max(0,b_t) (intermediate unchanged).
If b_s >0 and b_t <0, Δ = -1 (if b_s>0, b_t<0). If b_s>0, b_t>=0, Δ = -1 + (1 if b_t>=0? Actually max(0,b_t+1)-max(0,b_t) =1 if b_t>=0, 0 if b_t=-1? Let's compute: if b_t=-1, max(0,0)-max(0,-1)=0; if b_t<-1, 0-0=0; if b_t>=0, (b_t+1)-b_t=1. So Δ = -1 + [b_t>=0]. If b_s<=0, b_t<0, Δ = 0 + 0 =0 (since max(0,b_s-1)=0 if b_s<=0? if b_s=0, max(0,-1)=0, max(0,0)=0; if b_s<0,0). If b_s<=0, b_t>=0, Δ = 0 +1 =1 (bad). Thus only paths from positive to negative reduce objective by 1; paths from positive to nonnegative have net 0? Wait if b_s>0, b_t>=0, Δ = -1 +1 =0. So they don't reduce, they just shift positive from s to t. Paths from nonpositive to negative have Δ=0 (reduce negative but no positive change). Paths from nonpositive to nonpositive? If b_s<=0,b_t<0, Δ=0; if b_t>=0, Δ=1 (bad). So optimal g consists of paths from positive to negative, plus possibly paths from positive to nonnegative or nonpositive to negative that don't change objective but could enable more reductions? Since paths can be combined, any path from positive to negative can be decomposed directly; no need for intermediate transfers. The maximum reduction is max flow from positive to negative. This proof is solid.

```python
import sys
from collections import deque

sys.setrecursionlimit(1 << 25)

class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.g = [[] for _ in range(n)]
    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        self.g[fr].append([to, cap, len(self.g[to])])
        self.g[to].append([fr, 0, len(self.g[fr]) - 1])
    def bfs(self, s, t):
        self.level = [-1] * self.n
        q = deque([s])
        self.level[s] = 0
        while q:
            v = q.popleft()
            for e in self.g[v]:
                if e[1] > 0 and self.level[e[0]] < 0:
                    self.level[e[0]] = self.level[v] + 1
                    q.append(e[0])
        return self.level[t] >= 0
    def dfs(self, v, t, f):
        if v == t:
            return f
        for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.g[v])):
            self.it[v] = i
            e = self.g[v][i]
            if e[1] > 0 and self.level[e[0]] == self.level[v] + 1:
                ret = self.dfs(e[0], t, min(f, e[1]))
                if ret:
                    e[1] -= ret
                    self.g[e[0]][e[2]][1] += ret
                    return ret
        return 0
    def max_flow(self, s, t):
        flow = 0
        INF = 10**18
        while self.bfs(s, t):
            self.it = [0] * self.n
            while True:
                f = self.dfs(s, t, INF)
                if not f:
                    break
                flow += f
        return flow

def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return
    N = int(data[0])
    edges = []
    reach = [[False] * 26 for _ in range(26)]
    for i in range(26):
        reach[i][i] = True
    for i in range(N):
        s = data[1 + i]
        u = ord(s[0]) - ord('A')
        v = ord(s[1]) - ord('A')
        edges.append((u, v))
        reach[u][v] = True

    for k in range(26):
        for i in range(26):
            if reach[i][k]:
                for j in range(26):
                    if reach[k][j]:
                        reach[i][j] = True

    comp = [-1] * 26
    cid = 0
    for i in range(26):
        if comp[i] == -1:
            comp[i] = cid
            for j in range(26):
                if comp[j] == -1 and reach[i][j] and reach[j][i]:
                    comp[j] = cid
            cid += 1

    C = cid
    internal = [False] * C
    indeg = [0] * C
    outdeg = [0] * C
    inter_pairs = set()

    for u, v in edges:
        cu = comp[u]
        cv = comp[v]
        if cu == cv:
            internal[cu] = True
        else:
            outdeg[cu] += 1
            indeg[cv] += 1
            inter_pairs.add((cu, cv))

    b = [indeg[i] - outdeg[i] for i in range(C)]
    P0 = sum(x for x in b if x > 0)

    S = C
    T = C + 1
    dinic = Dinic(C + 2)
    INF = 10**9
    for i in range(C):
        if b[i] > 0:
            dinic.add_edge(S, i, b[i])
        elif b[i] < 0:
            dinic.add_edge(i, T, -b[i])
    for u, v in inter_pairs:
        dinic.add_edge(u, v, INF)

    M = dinic.max_flow(S, T)
    cover_inter = P0 - M

    isolated_internal = 0
    for i in range(C):
        if internal[i] and indeg[i] == 0 and outdeg[i] == 0:
            isolated_internal += 1

    print(cover_inter + isolated_internal)

if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```

=== ANSWER ===
```python
import sys
from collections import deque

sys.setrecursionlimit(1 << 25)


class Dinic:
    def __init__(self, n):
        self.n = n
        self.g = [[] for _ in range(n)]

    def add_edge(self, fr, to, cap):
        self.g[fr].append([to, cap, len(self.g[to])])
        self.g[to].append([fr, 0, len(self.g[fr]) - 1])

    def bfs(self, s, t):
        self.level = [-1] * self.n
        q = deque([s])
        self.level[s] = 0
        while q:
            v = q.popleft()
            for e in self.g[v]:
                if e[1] > 0 and self.level[e[0]] < 0:
                    self.level[e[0]] = self.level[v] + 1
                    q.append(e[0])
        return self.level[t] >= 0

    def dfs(self, v, t, f):
        if v == t:
            return f
        for i in range(self.it[v], len(self.g[v])):
            self.it[v] = i
            e = self.g[v][i]
            if e[1] > 0 and self.level[e[0]] == self.level[v] + 1:
                ret = self.dfs(e[0], t, min(f, e[1]))
                if ret:
                    e[1] -= ret
                    self.g[e[0]][e[2]][1] += ret
                    return ret
        return 0

    def max_flow(self, s, t):
        flow = 0
        INF = 10**18
        while self.bfs(s, t):
            self.it = [0] * self.n
            while True:
                f = self.dfs(s, t, INF)
                if not f:
                    break
                flow += f
        return flow


def main():
    data = sys.stdin.read().split()
    if not data:
        return

    N = int(data[0])
    edges = []

    reach = [[False] * 26 for _ in range(26)]
    for i in range(26):
        reach[i][i] = True

    for i in range(N):
        s = data[1 + i]
        u = ord(s[0]) - ord('A')
        v = ord(s[1]) - ord('A')
        edges.append((u, v))
        reach[u][v] = True

    # Transitive closure
    for k in range(26):
        for i in range(26):
            if reach[i][k]:
                for j in range(26):
                    if reach[k][j]:
                        reach[i][j] = True

    # SCC decomposition
    comp = [-1] * 26
    cid = 0
    for i in range(26):
        if comp[i] == -1:
            comp[i] = cid
            for j in range(26):
                if comp[j] == -1 and reach[i][j] and reach[j][i]:
                    comp[j] = cid
            cid += 1

    C = cid
    internal = [False] * C
    indeg = [0] * C
    outdeg = [0] * C
    inter_pairs = set()

    for u, v in edges:
        cu = comp[u]
        cv = comp[v]
        if cu == cv:
            internal[cu] = True
        else:
            outdeg[cu] += 1
            indeg[cv] += 1
            inter_pairs.add((cu, cv))

    # b[v] = indeg - outdeg on the inter-SCC DAG
    b = [indeg[i] - outdeg[i] for i in range(C)]
    base = sum(x for x in b if x > 0)

    # Max flow from positive b vertices to negative b vertices
    S = C
    T = C + 1
    dinic = Dinic(C + 2)
    INF = 10**9

    for i in range(C):
        if b[i] > 0:
            dinic.add_edge(S, i, b[i])
        elif b[i] < 0:
            dinic.add_edge(i, T, -b[i])

    for u, v in inter_pairs:
        dinic.add_edge(u, v, INF)

    flow = dinic.max_flow(S, T)
    ans_inter = base - flow

    # Isolated SCCs that have internal edges need one string each
    isolated_internal = 0
    for i in range(C):
        if internal[i] and indeg[i] == 0 and outdeg[i] == 0:
            isolated_internal += 1

    print(ans_inter + isolated_internal)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    main()
```
